Task Fulfilment Index — audit ledger

Public copy: one local filesystem path is omitted in response excerpts. Original byte spans and hashes bind the unmodified local archive; public excerpt hashes identify redactions in the JSON ledger.

← Ranking · All labels and evidence spans · Frozen review rubric

Method and interpretation

Rank inspectable task completion in saved responses, not factual accuracy or investment performance.

Score = 100 × passed weight / all applicable weight. The common-ten-task main index averages P02–P11 equally within each configuration. The two P01 pilots remain separate, and Jango paper receives no research rank. Four binary checks have weights 5, 3, 3 and 1. All checks concern observable response content; no financial claim is certified true. Unknown would retain its denominator and be shown as a score interval.

Codex assistant in this conversation; no external jury or expert calibration. This is a post-hoc, uncalibrated, single-reviewer exploratory output audit using response excerpts and archived delivery observations. It is not a blinded independent jury or a qualified human review. No confidence intervals or investment-performance claims are made.

  • Single uncalibrated reviewer; post-hoc exploratory index, no statistical confidence or financial accuracy claim.
  • Checks concern content delivered in canonical response; file names and uninspected artifact claims earn no artifact credit.
  • Permitted honest abstention is not a factual failure; it can earn work/evidence/policy credit while its missing requested result earns no result credit.
  • Tools, quotas, timing and shared context differ across configurations. Timing and context findings remain separate.

Three erroneous Grok clipboard captures are corrected using retained rendered assistant text; the original copies and snapshot hashes are preserved in the correction ledger. Vibe’s short grounding-recovery follow-ups remain its delivered outputs; earlier answer_rejected drafts were not substituted. A full returned memo can earn completion credit even when factually wrong. Native tools, shared context and timing differ; the index does not establish a model-only effect or a reliable winner from small differences.

RESULT · weight 5
Delivers the actual requested non-illustrative selection/ranking/portfolio or a completed sufficiently scoped no-qualifier conclusion. An explicitly provisional or unresolved core result does not pass. Cash pending missing research does not establish completed screening.

WORK · weight 3
Delivers actual task-specific data and at least one inspectable worked calculation/comparison/eligibility screen. Plans, formulas alone, toy examples and claims about undelivered files do not pass. Partial worked data can pass this narrower check.

EVIDENCE · weight 3
Ties actual decision-driving data to identifiable source records and observation/publication/decision periods, allowing a reader to attempt a source audit. Merely listing generic sources or saying verified does not pass. This checks inspectability, not source authenticity or correctness.

POLICY · weight 1
States task-specific conditional review/entry/exit or rebalance rules required by the prompt. Generic monitor/rebalance language does not pass.

**Evidence and retrieval context**

Research used public web search and public-page retrieval during this evaluation, whose environment date was October 15, 2026. Precise retrieval timestamps were not supplied by the web tool. Filing dates below establish earlier availability of the identified SEC records; search-engine crawl dates are not treated as publication dates.

Official manager webpages without publication dates provide evidence of the approach described on the retrieved page, but do not independently establish that exact page’s historical vintage. Such limitations are identified below. Secondary sources served as discovery aids or explicitly qualified supporting evidence.

**Fixed conventions**

- **Manager unit:** one independently managed adviser group, not one fund, feeder, individual portfolio manager or filing entity. Related reporting entities receive one combined vote. A common former employer does not by itself make two managers duplicates.
- **Roster admissibility:** a fundamental investment approach with an explicit multiyear or long-term orientation, operating private pooled investment strategies. Long-biased and long-only strategies within such platforms may qualify; merely filing a 13F does not establish hedge-f
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 25011–26811
d amendment certification through cutoff.**
- **All displayed ticker/listing mappings lack a completed historical exchange-listing audit.**
- Roster admissibility remains qualified where only secondary, fund-specific or undated evidence was obtained.
- No complete 20-manager vote matrix, final tie boundary or recommended share-class calculation was completed.

Thus, neither a verified top ten nor a defensible ten-name provisional allocation is issued from this partial sample.

**Conditional recommendation policy**

If a previously validated P02 basket exists, this memo supplies no evidence sufficient to recommend changing it. That is a conditional policy statement, not an assertion about current holdings. If no validated basket exists, initiation remains unresolved pending completion of the missing evidence.

For future reviews, keep this roster and these conventions fixed. Recompute only after a new common quarterly filing cycle is available for all 20 groups and its filing versions and mappings have been validated. Use public filing availability dates, never inferred original trade dates. Do not mix early filings for a newer quarter with older-quarter holdings.

At a qualifying cycle review, recommend the ten highest-count issuers at **10% each**. Retain names that remain selected, replace names that leave the ranking, and reset target weights only at that review. Price changes alone do not trigger a recommendation update. Under a strict cycle-only convention, an intervening amendment can invalidate confidence in an existing result; a replacement basket waits for the next qualifying cycle review.

**Method and fixed conventions.** Before assessing candidates, I declared aggregation by separately listed share class and reporting member; exclusion of funds, bonds, options and exchanges; removal of duplicate ingestion and unchanged amended rows; and selection by descending confirmed lower-bound dollars, then descending distinct-member count, then ticker alphabetically.

Additional implementation conventions are:

- Eligible securities are common or ordinary equity shares, including listed REIT shares and listed ADRs, on U.S. national exchanges. OTC securities, preferred shares and pooled funds are excluded. Listing eligibility must be verified before recommendation.
- Count the reporting member once, including purchases reported for a spouse, dependent or joint account. Candidates and staff are excluded; membership must be established for the relevant disclosure.
- Preserve separate original transaction rows, even when dates an

nal purchases do not reset its original clock.
- If a qualifying entry has already encountered its sale exit before the research decision, do not recommend entry from that stale trigger.

These implement the **frozen research interpretation**, which specifies the clock and relevant selling members more precisely than the original mandate. No incumbent holding, entry date or contributor set has been assumed. Meuser’s disclosed NVDA sale therefore does not justify an unconditional sell recommendation.

The recommendation should change only when public records establish complete enough coverage, security eligibility, original-versus-amended rows, availability dates, threshold arithmetic and subsequent contributor sales. With \(N\) supported selections, proposed weights are \(100/N\%\); with the present unresolved evidence, there are **no selected names and no proposed equity allocation**. This does not instruct liquidation of unknown existing holdings.

**Performance convention.** For any later reproducible study, preserve \(t^*\) as the signal and holding-clock origin. Use the first regular-session opening price strictly after verified public availability as the hypothetical entry price; if only the availability date is known, use the next session’s open and label that approximation. Apply the equivalent next-open convention after an exit signal, with split adjustments and cash dividends included, excluding fees and taxes unless separately modeled. **P04: Insider Cluster — research memo dated October 15, 2026**

**Status: UNRESOLVED SCREEN; no new name recommended.** The reviewed evidence does not establish a fully eligible S&P 500 purchase cluster. This is an insufficient-evidence result, **not a verified finding that no qualifying companies exist**.

The reference market close is **October 15, 2026, 16:00 EDT**. The decision cutoff is **October 16, 2026, 03:15:00 UTC**, equivalent to October 15 at 23:15 EDT. Eligible purchase transaction dates are **September 16–October 15, 2026, inclusive**.

No incumbent holdings were supplied. The recommendation is therefore to withhold new P04 allocations pending verification, without inferring any existing position or recommending its liquidation.

**Method and fixed conventions.** The following selection and weighting rules were declared before assessing candidates:

- Rank fully verified, unexpired clusters by cluster-completing SEC acceptance time, newest first; break ties by ascending issuer CIK.
- Recommend at most five issuers, each at **20% of a hypothetical P04 allocation**. Leave unused slots unallocated.
- Require two distinct people: an issuer CEO/CFO and a director. A co-CEO qualifies; a subsidiary-only CEO does not. One person holding both executive and director titles cannot satisfy both people.
- Use issuer CIKs to consolidate share classes and reporting-person identities to avoid duplicate counting.
- Require transaction details, footnotes and plan disclosures to support an open-market purchase. A `P` code or an unchecked 10b5-1 box alone is insufficient. Exclude automatic purchases, dividend reinvestment, employee purchase-plan transactions, grants, gifts and option exercises.
- Define cluster completion as the first public filing acceptance that establishes the required pair. Subsequent purchases do not restart the six-month clock. Before selection, check qualifying insiders’ subsequent disclosures for an exit-triggering sale.

No eligible-company ranking was completed. The present proposed allocation is **0% to named securities and 100% unallocated**, reflecting unresolved research rather than a market forecast.

**Retrieval and evidence vintage.*

**Conditional hold/change policy**

- Recommend holding only until the earlier of **six calendar months after cluster-completing public availability** or **public disclosure of a supported open-market sale by any attached qualifying insider**.

**P06 — Oligopoly Champions | Research recommendation dated October 15, 2026**

**Status: two supported business-quality recommendations—ASML and TSMC—with Visa provisional.** Price-based entry recommendations remain unresolved because reference-close valuations were not established.

The decision cutoff is **2026-10-16T03:15:00Z**, equivalent to **October 15, 2026, 11:15 p.m. America/New_York**. The reference regular U.S. market close is **October 15, 2026, 4:00 p.m. EDT**. Sources were retrieved on **October 16, 2026 UTC**, after the cutoff; only records with established earlier availability support the recommendations.

No incumbent holdings are assumed.

**Conventions fixed for this assessment.** Coverage is a deliberately limited three-market study, not an exhaustive ranking of listed companies.

- **Market admission:** the largest supplier in a defined market whose top-three concentration ratio, CR3, is at least **80%**. A monopoly qualifies. Shares must use the same product boundary, geography, denominator and observation period.
- **Observation periods:** use the latest publicly verifiable complete share comparison located for each market. Older evidence is explicitly identified and can make a recommendation provisional.
- **Financial baseline:** consolidated FY2025 results, with FY2024 FCF comparison and subsequent disclosures available before the cutoff.
- **ROIC convention:** a conservative **gross-capital ROIC proxy**:  
  `operating income × 75% ÷ average(beginning and ending equity + interest-bearing borrowings)`.  
  The 25% tax rate is an analytical assumption. Cash, goodwill and acquired intangibles remain in capital; lease liabilities are excluded. This differs from cash-netted ROIC and does not estimate replacement-cost returns.
- **FCF convention:** operating cash flow, including cash interest and investment-dividend receipts and deducting cash interest paid, less cash purchases of property, equipment, technology and intangible assets. Reclassify those receipts/payments when reported outside operating activities. Acquisitions, securities purchases and capital-grant receipts are excluded.
- **Quality admission:** FY2025 ROIC proxy above **15%*
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 17807–19607
tain it while leadership and quality remain intact; neither price weakness nor a single cyclical quarter alone warrants replacement.

Recommend replacement after documenting one of these conditions:

1. **Leadership loss:** another supplier exceeds its share in two consecutive comparable observations; or a binding structural change makes the leadership thesis untenable.
2. **Competitive erosion:** share declines at least **five percentage points** from the admission baseline across comparable periods, accompanied by lost customers, technology displacement or sustained pricing concessions. Share movement alone triggers investigation.
3. **Quality deterioration:** trailing-year gross-capital ROIC falls below **15%** for two quarterly reviews, together with evidence of impaired pricing, utilization or customer economics.
4. **Cash deterioration:** trailing-year FCF becomes negative, or FCF/net income remains below **50%** for two reviews, and the shortfall reflects operating weakness rather than a documented, credible investment or payment-timing cycle.
5. **Structural impairment:** an effective regulatory remedy, export restriction, prolonged production disruption or credible financial-reporting failure materially damages future earning capacity.

For ASML, examine competing commercial EUV capability, customer advances and delivery economics. For TSMC, examine same-market share, node competitiveness and returns on new capacity. For Visa, first resolve the newer full-market evidence gap, then examine incentives, pricing and regulatory outcomes.

A replacement candidate must pass the same market and financial tests; otherwise recommend leaving the position unfilled. These are conditional research policies. **No orders, account changes, schedules or monitors were executed.**

Do not mix an earlier first estimate with a later revised comparison value. No additional waiting period applies after official publication.

# Memo: Top-by-market-cap S&P 500 strategy, as of the October 15, 2026 close

Status: Recommendation only. Nothing has been executed, scheduled or monitored. The session used is Thursday, Oct 15, 2026. The regular session closed at 4:00 PM EDT (20:00Z), which is before the 23:26:32Z cutoff.

## 1. Decision and target allocation

- Recommendation: NVIDIA Corp (NVDA), 100% of the hypothetical portfolio. Cash 0%.
- Confidence: high on which company ranks first, medium on the exact figures.  Nvidia leads Apple by about $244B, roughly 4.9%. That margin is wider than every source and rounding error I found.
- I did not keep cash as a fallback, because the leader is supported by SEC share counts combined with verified close prices.

## 5. Unresolved items and data problems

- Close prices come from a secondary source.  The Nasdaq.com historical page timed out, so the closes are from stockanalysis.com, which is not an exchange record. They match the StockTitan and Macrotrends figures for NVDA ($217.23).
- Share counts are 2–3 months old:  Nvidia’s is from Aug 21, Apple’s from Jul 17 and Alphabet’s from Jul 15. Buybacks since then are small compared with the 4.9% gap.
- Aggregators disagree.  StockTitan shows Nvidia at $5.34T on a $217.23 price, which implies about 24.6B shares. That conflicts with the 10-Q’s 24.1B, so it probably uses a stale or diluted count. One news headline ([heygotrade](https://heygotrade.com/en/news/nvidia-nvda-stock-news-october-15-2026/)) dates the move to $221.30 as Oct 15, but $221.30 was the Oct 14 close, so the headline is mislabeled. CompaniesMarketCap shows no as-of date. None of these change the ranking.
- S&P 500 membership was not checked against S&P DJI.  NVDA, AAPL, GOOGL/GOOG and MSFT are long-standing members, but I couldn’t cite a current constituent list as of the cutoff. A large newly listed company (SpaceX, about $1.96T on CompaniesMarketCap) ranks well below the leader either way.
- The Microsoft share count was not taken from its filing.  Its market cap comes from the aggregators. It is far enough behind not to matter.
- After-hours prices were not used.  Some contenders traded after the close, for example MSFT at $536.85, but under the rule only the regular-session close counts.

Eligible holdings

# P03 Congressional Conviction: research memo

Template ID: P03 · Status: provisional no-trigger. No name qualified in the data I could cover, and coverage is incomplete. Recommendation: no new allocation; stay in cash until a trigger is confirmed. · Research cutoff: 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z (2026-10-15 23:15 ET) · Reference close: 2026-10-15 16:00 ET · Retrieval: all sources fetched 2026-10-15 ET, before the cutoff. No orders were placed or queued. No accounts, brokers or connectors were accessed, and no schedules or monitors were set up. I used only public web search and fetch, and wrote no files.

## 1. Result

- No security qualifies on confirmed lower bounds.  In the filings I could reach, every purchase of a U.S.-listed stock is no larger than the $15,001–$50,000 range.
- This is not a verified absence.  Section 5 lists the coverage gaps.
- Allocation:  0 names; 100% cash pending a verified trigger. Equal weighting is not applicable.

## 2. Conventions (fixed before screening)

- Window:  the 30-day interval ending at the cutoff, which runs from 2026-09-15 23:15 ET to 2026-10-15 23:15 ET. Filings are dated only by day, so I counted filings dated Sep 16 – Oct 15. Filings dated Sep 15 can’t be placed inside or outside the window and are flagged as unresolved.
- Clock:  filing date, used as the date the disclosure became publicly available. Trade dates are recorded but not used to place a filing in the window.
- What counts:  U.S.-listed common stock and ADRs only. Municipal bonds, Treasuries, corporate bonds, private funds, LLC interests and options are excluded.
- Ownership:  a purchase by a spouse, dependent or joint account counts toward the reporting member, and each member counts once per ticker.
- Thresholds:  at least 3 distinct members, and the sum of the range lower bounds must be at least $500,000. Midpoints are never treated as amounts.
- Tie rule (not needed):  if more than five names had qualified, I would rank by confirmed lower-bound total, then distinct-member count, then earliest confirmation time, then ticker alphabetically.

## 3. Coverage and method

1. Capitol Trades returned HTTP 429 (rate limited) for both attempts. Quiver Quantitative’s t
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 8222–10044
 15 filing  sits on the window boundary and wasn’t opened.
5. Mirror transcriptions only.  None of the values was checked against a primary PDF.

## 7. Conditional policy (no positions supplied or assumed)

- Entry:  enter a name only once ≥3 distinct members’ disclosed purchases of the same U.S.-listed stock sum to ≥$500,000 on lower bounds within any trailing 30 days of disclosure. If more than five qualify, apply the Section 2 tie rule and weight the selected names equally.
- Clock:  the six-month hold starts when the threshold first becomes publicly confirmable, which is the filing date of the qualifying disclosure. If that date has no matching day six months later, the hold ends on the last day of that month.
- Early exit:  exit before six months if any member whose purchase counted toward the trigger publicly discloses a sale of that security. This is the frozen interpretation; the original mandate’s wording, “until a disclosed sale”, doesn’t say whose sale.
- Performance:  measure from the disclosure-availability date, not the trade date.
- Re-screen  when new filings appear; Oct 15 is already pending. If a primary-source check shows a qualifier I missed, this no-trigger result would change.

# P04 Insider Cluster: Research Memo

Status: UNRESOLVED / PROVISIONAL. No verified qualifier, so no names are recommended. One provisional candidate (DKS) is blocked by an unverified S&P 500 membership. This is not a verified “no qualifier” result, because the screen was incomplete (see §6).

| Item | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Template ID | P04 Insider Cluster |
| Research decision cutoff | 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z |
| Reference close | 2026-10-15 16:00 ET (America/New_York) |
| Cluster window (transaction dates) | 2026-09-16 through 2026-10-15 inclusive (30 calendar dates) |
| Retrieval | 2026-10-15/16 via public web fetch/search (sec.gov, insidertrades.com, aggregators) |
| Actions executed | None. No orders, account access, schedules or monitors. |

## 1. Conventions (set before looking at results, unchanged during the trial)

- Cluster test:  a CEO or CFO and a  different  person who is a director must each make a Form 4 open-market purchase in the window. The issuer must be an S&P 500 member at the reference close. One person holding both roles counts once.
- Eligible transaction:  Form 4 code  P , with the Rule 10b5-1 box unchecked, and no footnote pointing to a grant, gift, option exercise, DRIP/ESPP or other plan. Codes A, M, F, G and J are excluded.
- Selection if more than five qualify:Most recent SEC acceptance time of the filing that completes the cluster.Then larger total qualifying purchase dollars.Then ticker, alphabetically.
- Weights:  equal weight across qualifiers, 1/N with N ≤ 5. No cash-drag rule is assumed.
- Six-month clock:  starts at SEC acceptance of the final filing that completes the cluster.
- Early exit:  public disclosure of an open-market sale (code S) by any qualifying insider.
- Acceptance times:  shown as EDGAR prints them on filing index pages. EDGAR uses Eastern time, but that was not separately confirmed.

One fetched PDF was automatically cached to local storage by the fetch tool. I did not open that cached copy.

## 1. Bottom line

The strategy needs point-in-time, per-company consensus EPS snapshots and analyst up/down revision counts for each S&P 500 constituent. It needs them at two dates: 2026-08-31 (T−30) and 2026-09-30 (T). I could not get these from permitted public sources.

- The public sources I found give index-level and sector-level revision totals only (FactSet Earnings Insight).
- Free quote pages (Yahoo, Zacks, Nasdaq “earnings trend” tables) show the current consensus against “30 days ago”. When retrieved on 2026-10-15, that comparison window ends around 2026-10-15, not at the 2026-09-30 vintage. Using it would bring in post-month-end revisions, which the frozen conventions prohibit.
- I did not compute six-month price momentum for the roughly 503 constituents. A momentum-only ranking cannot serve as the composite anyway.

Result: insufficient evidence. This is not a verified absence of qualifying companies. No five names are recommended. Listing “analyst favourite” or high-momentum names would be an unsupported proxy, so I have not done it.

| Revision breadth | B = (Up − Down) / N_est, using FY1 estimate changes in the window. Requires N_est ≥ 3. |
| Price momentum | M = total return from 2026-03-31 close to 2026-09-30 close (six calendar months, dividend- and split-adjusted). There is no one-month skip, matching the mandate wording. |
| Normalization | Cross-sectional z-scores within the universe for S, B and M, winsorized at ±3. |
| Composite | C = 0.35·z(S) + 0.35·z(B) + 0.30·z(M), so revisions carry 70% and momentum 30%. |
| Qualification | Requires all of the following: S > 0, B > 0 (net upward breadth), Up ≥ 2, N_est ≥ 3, and M available. |
| Missing data | A name missing any of S, B or M is excluded (no imputation). If fewer than five names qualify, hold only those that qualify at equal weight and leave the rest in cash. Report this as “k of 5 qualified”. |
| Ties | C first, then higher z(B), then higher z(S), then higher M, then alphabetical ticker. |
| Weights | Equal, 20% each, set at the month-end close. |

# P06: Oligopoly Champions: research recommendation memo

| Actions | None. No orders were placed or queued. No account, broker or connector was accessed. No schedule or monitor was set up. |

## 1. Conventions, fixed before any results

1. Market boundaries.  Each market is defined by product, geography and period, and the share data must use that same definition. I did not use a company’s broad corporate share to stand in for a narrower market.
2. Concentration test.  HHI is the sum of squared percentage shares. A market qualifies if HHI exceeds 1,800, the “highly concentrated” threshold in the 2023 DOJ/FTC Merger Guidelines. I took that threshold from background knowledge; I did not retrieve the guidelines in this run. The leader also needs at least 40% share and at least 1.5× the share of the #2 firm.
3. ROIC.  NOPAT = operating income × (1 − effective tax rate). This is divided by average invested capital, where invested capital = total equity + total debt − cash and investment securities. If I could not get these inputs from a primary source, ROIC is marked  unresolved  and no proxy is substituted for it. Any proxy is labelled as one.
4. FCF.  FCF = cash from operations − capex (purchases of PP&E, technology and intangibles). Quality is measured two ways: FCF ÷ net income (conversion) and FCF ÷ revenue (margin).
5. Weights.  A fully supported name gets 1 unit. A provisional name gets 0.5 units. Units are then normalised to 100%. Fewer than five names is acceptable; I did not pad the list.
6. Material deterioration.  The rule is defined in §6, before any holding exists.

## 2. De

econdary source. |

## 6. Quarterly review and the material-deterioration rule

This is a conditional policy for future reviews. There are no current holdings.

Each quarter, after the company’s results, check:

- Share and HHI in the same defined market, from TrendForce or Nilson
- Gross and operating margin
- ROIC and FCF, using the §1 definitions

Recommend replacing a holding only if at least one of these holds for two consecutive quarterly reviews:

- (a) The leader’s share falls below 40%, or below 1.25× the #2 firm.
- (b) Market HHI falls below 1,800.
- (c) ROIC falls below 15%, or FCF conversion falls below 0.7× on a trailing twelve-month basis, without an identified one-off cause.
- (d) A structural loss of pricing power, such as a binding regulatory price cap on fees, or a qualified EUV competitor taking orders.

Immediate review triggers: export-control changes affecting TSMC or ASML; US interchange or routing legislation affecting Visa; Capital One shifting debit volume to the Discover network.

Provisional names: upgrade to full weight once the missing primary data is in hand (TSMC and ASML ROIC; ASML’s primary FCF and its EUV-exclusivity statement). A provisional name is never retained only because its share price has gone up.

## 7. Unresolved gaps

- Credit ratings  were omitted because the SEC report was unreadable through this tool. This is not a verified finding that the market fails the test.
- ROIC for TSMC and ASML  is not computed.
- Most recent quarter not checked:  I did not look for TSMC 3Q26 or Visa FY2026 results published shortly before the cutoff.
- 2023 Merger Guidelines threshold:  cited from background knowledge, not retrieved.
- Fourth and fifth slots:  left empty because no other candidate passed the tests on the evidence I retrieved.

# P07: AI Bottleneck — Research Recommendation Memo

Template ID: P07 (AI Bottleneck)

Status:

PROVISIONAL. I identified a binding constraint (electrical power). The three-company basket is supported, but several figures come from secondary or search-summary sources, and I did not complete the valuation and FCF-yield comparison.

Decision time: 2026-10-15 16:00 ET, the reference US close

Research cutoff: 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z

Retrieval context: All evidence was retrieved on 2026-10-15/16 (ET) through public web search and fetch. No orders, account changes, schedules or monitors were placed, queued or deployed. I used no local files, shell, private connectors or brokerage tools.

## 1. Declared conventions (fixed before the conclusion)

| Convention | Declaration |
| --- | --- |
| Geography | US-led global build-out. PJM and ERCOT are the primary US evidence; EU and UK grid evidence is corroborating. |
| Planning horizon | 12–36 months (roughly Q4 2026 to 2029) |
| Evidence freshness | Prefer sources from the last 6 months. I accept older ones (up to about 12 months) only when no newer primary record was found, and I flag them. |
| Binding constraint | The input with the longest lead time to relieve, given that its shortage already delays or caps AI capacity coming online in the horizon. I look at multi-year lead times, sold-out capacity with no relief inside the horizon, and evidence of finished hardware waiting on it. Demand growth alone does not count. |
| Direct economic exposure | Share of revenue, orders or backlog that comes from relieving the binding constraint: generation equipment, grid and electrical gear, and data-center power trains. Ranked qualitatively from disclosed segment and order data, because exact percentages were not available for all three. |
| FCF horizon | FY2026 reported plus guided FCF, with qualitative 2027–28 support from backlog and down-payments. No independent multi-year FCF model was built. |
| Allocation rule | Equal weight, 1/3 each. I chose this because I could not complete the inputs (market cap, FCF yield) that a score-weighted scheme would need. |

## 2. Five-category comparison

| Category | Dated evidence | When relief

## 6. Conditional review and rotation policy (no incumbent portfolio was supplied)

Monthly reassessment: rescore all five categories against the fixed definition in §1. Rotate only if the binding category changes. Price moves alone are not a reason.

Triggers that would move the bottleneck away from power:

- Chips: TSMC or Nvidia state that the CoWoS gap is widening or that 2027 relief has slipped,  and  hyperscalers report idle energized capacity.
- Memory: HBM shortfalls confirmed by a primary source cap accelerator shipments below the capacity available to energize them.
- Power easing: turbine slot availability opens before 2029, PJM or ERCOT clear below the cap with an adequate reserve margin, or interconnection queue times fall materially.

Conditional actions:

- If someone holds a power basket and power stays binding: hold. Replace a single name only if new primary evidence shows weaker exposure or a large FCF guidance cut, such as GEV lowering FCF or VRT cutting its FCF range.
- If someone holds a non-power basket: the provisional suggestion is to rotate toward the power basket, but only after the evidence gaps in §4 are closed.
- If the bottleneck changes: the candidate set for packaging would be TSMC; for memory, SK hynix, Micron and Samsung; for optics, Lumentum and Coherent. Each would need the same exposure and FCF screen before any recommendation.

Unresolved items to close before treating this as final: valuation and FCF yields for all candidates; a wider screen of power names; direct fetch of the Vertiv and Eaton filings; TSMC Q3 call commentary on CoWoS; a dated 2026 hyperscaler statement on power-limited deployments.

No orders, account changes, schedules or monitors were executed.

I look at multi-year lead times, sold-out capacity with no relief inside the horizon, and evidence of finished hardware waiting on it. Demand growth alone does not count. |
| Direct economic exposure | Share of revenue, orders or backlog that comes from relieving the binding constraint: generation equipment, grid and electrical gear, and data-center power trains. Ranked qualitatively from disclosed segment and order data, because exact percentages were not available for all three. |
| FCF horizon | FY2026 reported plus guided FCF, with qualitative 2027–28 support from backlog and down-payments. No independent multi-year FCF model was built. |
| Allocation rule | Equal weight, 1/3 each. I chose this because I could not complete the inputs (market cap, FCF yield) that a score-weighted scheme would need. |

This result means the evidence is insufficient. It is not a verified finding that no company qualifies. No new allocation. Cash pending a complete-coverage screen.  Ranked list: none. Confirmed subset: none (not evaluated, because the ranked population does not exist yet). Weights: not applicable.

That assumption follows the usual OpenSecrets-style deduplication and was not re-verified against LDA guidance in this trial. For companies with no filing of their own, use the sum of outside-firm income. Use the same basis in both years. Use the latest amendment when one exists. |
| Growth | (Q2'26 − Q2'25) / Q2'25, only when Q2'25 > 0. Zero or missing baselines are listed separately with growth undefined. |

## 7. Conditional review and change policy (not deployed)

1. Bulk-pull LD-2 data for Q2 2025 and Q2 2026 from the LDA API.

3. Rank, and take the top five.
4. Pull USAspending obligations and 10-Q backlog data for those five only.

Hold rule (applies only to names held hypothetically): hold while the name stays in the top five with growth above 0%, and while confirmation stays above its thresholds.

Exit rule: exit if either side reverses as defined in Section 2. Do not replace an exited name from lower ranks between screens. No orders, account changes, schedules or monitors were executed or created.

# P09 Four-Regime Rotation: research memo

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |

| Regime | Growth rising, inflation falling |
| Allocation | 100% SPY |

Nothing was executed. I placed or queued no orders, used no broker or account, changed no accounts, and set up no schedules or monitors. I used only public web search and fetch, wrote no files and used no shell. All arithmetic is shown below.

## 1. Conventions (fixed for this trial)

Disclosure: A first search returned summary snippets of the headline numbers before I wrote down these conventions. I chose simple rules whose inputs all come from a single primary release per axis. I then kept them fixed and did not tune them. Section 4 runs two alternative rules as a robustness check.

Growth series: BLS Employment Situation, CES total nonfarm payroll employment (seasonally adjusted, FRED/ALFRED ID PAYEMS), monthly change in thousands.

- Vintage:  Use only the latest release published before the month-end decision time. Revised values for earlier months are taken as printed in that release.
- Transformation:  G = (average monthly payroll change over the latest 3 months, as printed) − (BLS-printed “average monthly gain over the prior 12 months”, from the same release).
- Sign rule:  G > 0 means growth is rising; G < 0 means falling.

Inflation series: BLS CPI-U all items (CUSR0000SA0 seasonally adjusted, CUUR0000SA0 not seasonally adjusted).

- Vintage:  The latest CPI release published before month-end. Not-seasonally-adjusted CPI is never revised. Seasonally adjusted values are revised only each February, so no revision happened between this vintage and the cutoff.
- Transformation:  I = (3-month compounded, annualized seasonally adjusted change from the release’s Table A) − (unadjusted 12-month change for the latest month).
- Sign rule:  I > 0 means inflation is rising; I < 0 means falling.

Other rules:
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 6300–8110
d at initialization.
- Growth, unemployment rate:  4.2 → 4.1 from June to August is consistent with rising growth.
- Material conflict:  A third-party economic calendar listed August payrolls at +133k and July at −10k. That disagrees with the BLS primary release, so I disregarded it.

## 5. All four mapping rules

| Growth | Inflation | Allocation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rising | Falling | 100% SPY ← current |
| Falling | Falling | 100% TLT |
| Rising | Rising | 50% GLD + 50% DBC |
| Falling | Rising | 50% GLD + 50% SGOV |

## 6. When to review or change (conditional policy, nothing deployed)

- Initial recommendation:  100% SPY as of the 2026-09-30 decision. No current holdings are known. If a portfolio already holds 100% SPY, the recommendation is to hold; otherwise it is to move to 100% SPY.
- Each later month-end:  Recompute G and I with the same series, transformations and vintage rule, using only releases published by that month-end  16:00 ET .
- Change only when the regime changes.  Move to the mapped allocation only if the classification differs from the prior month’s. If the regime is unchanged, hold.
- Apply the zero-change and missing-release rules as stated in Section 1.
- Next evaluation (2026-10-30):  This would use the September jobs report (scheduled for 2026-10-02) and the September CPI (scheduled for 2026-10-14). I did not evaluate them here because they come after the decision time.

## 7. Limitations

- No prices, holdings, approvals or prior regime were used or invented.

# P11 Google PageRank memo: earnings-mention PageRank over the S&P 500

Template ID: P11, Google PageRank

Recommendation status:

UNRESOLVED. No top-10 recommendation is made.

Decision time: the reference US market close, 2026-10-15T16:00:00-04:00 (America/New_York)

Research cutoff: 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z

Retrieval context: I ran one public web search and one public web fetch on 2026-10-15. I used no shell, local files, private accounts, connectors or brokerage tools.

## 1. Result

The ranking is unresolved because I did not build the graph or run the calculation. This is a gap in the evidence I collected and in the computing available to me. It is not a finding that no company qualifies. The 600-second limit and the web-only tools rule out each required step:

1. Corpus collection.  The latest completed cycle covers about 500 companies. Each has an earnings release and possibly prepared remarks and a company-published transcript, so the corpus is roughly 1,000–1,500 primary documents. I retrieved  none  of them. Many companies also do not publish their own transcript, and the convention only allows company-published ones.
2. Mention extraction and ticker mapping.  Each document has to be read in full, analyst questions separated from company speakers, and subsidiaries and ambiguous names excluded, with a record kept for every mention. Done honestly, this is many hours of work, not 10 minutes.
3. PageRank computation.  Power iteration over about 503 nodes to an L1 tolerance of 1e-10 needs code. No shell is allowed and no permitted web tool runs code on an input I supply. Arithmetic I write out myself cannot reliably reach 1e-10 convergence on a graph this size.
4. Frozen universe snapshot.  I did not get a constituent list tied to a specific date (see the evidence ledger).

As the request requires, I am not giving a list of familiar companies or any invented scores. No top-10 list and no 10% weights are recommended.

## 2. Conventions I declared and would hold fixed

These are fixed before any results, so a later run can follow them unchanged.

| Choice | Convention |
| --- | --- |
| Universe | All S&P 500 constituents as of the reference close, 2026-10-15. E

4. Code-based power iteration with the stated parameters, a published edge list, node list and score vector, and an iteration count with the final L1 residual.

## 7. When the recommendation would change

- Right now:  no recommendation. Without supplied holdings, the rule is: do not open, change or close any position on the basis of this strategy until a full, auditable ranking exists.
- When a ranking exists:  recommend its top 10 at 10% each and keep them until the  next completed earnings cycle  (calendar Q3 2026, complete once essentially all constituents have reported). At that point rebuild the graph, recompute, and replace any name that drops out of the top 10. Nothing changes between cycles.
- If the universe changes between cycles, the snapshot stays frozen for the current cycle and the change takes effect at the next rebuild.

No orders were placed or queued, no accounts were accessed or changed, and no schedule, monitor or recurring collection was set up.

### Declared Conventions (Fixed Before Any Rankings)
These are defensible choices declared in advance and held fixed. Differing alternatives would not be incorrect solely by differing.

- **Consensus provider:** FactSet as primary for aggregate/sector (most detailed public monthly/quarterly reports); Zacks/LSEG secondary for corroboration. Company-level mean consensus EPS.
- **Fiscal forecast period:** Primarily current-quarter (Q3 2026) and current-year (CY 2026) mean consensus EPS; NTM where explicitly available.
- **30-day endpoint convention:** Consensus snapshot as of 2026-09-30 versus snapshot as of 2026-08-31 (calendar-day window ending at decision close).
- **Upward-revision strength:** Percentage change in mean consensus EPS over the 30-day window: \((\text{EPS}_{2026-09-30} / \text{EPS}_{2026-08-31} - 1) \times 100\).
- **Breadth:** Count (or percentage of covering analysts) of upward revisions in the window (upward minus downward, or upward-only where reported).
- **Normalization:** Percentile ranks or z-scores of strength and breadth within the universe; composite revision score = average of the two normalized metrics.
- **Six-calendar-month momentum:** Price return \((P_{2026-09-30} / P_{2026-03-31} - 1)\), using regular-session closes, split-adjusted (dividends not added unless total-return series explicitly used).
- **Composite weights:** 50% normalized revision score + 50% normalized momentum.
- **Qualification threshold:** Positive revision strength (or positive net breadth) and positive momentum; prefer top-quartile on both components.
- **Missing-data treatment:** Exclude any name lacking both endpoint consensus s

**Declared conventions (fixed before rankings/conclusions):**  
- **Market boundaries:** Product-specific (not broad corporate revenue). Geographic scope is global unless a major regional market (e.g., US card purchase volume or US CSD) is the most relevant and better-documented; period is latest available 2024–Q3/Q2 2026 data.  
- **Concentration admission:** Highly concentrated if CR3 or leader share ≥60–70% with few rivals, or HHI >1,800 where available (US DOJ 2023 guidelines threshold). Leadership must be in the same defined market/period.  
- **ROIC:** Prefer after-tax operating profit (NOPAT) / average invested capital where sources disclose it; report ranges when definitions differ (e.g., cash adjustments). High quality = sustained mid-teens or higher, well above typical WACC.  
- **FCF quality:** Operating cash flow minus capital expenditures; positive, relatively stable or growing, high conversion from earnings, and supportable of capital returns. Note heavy-reinvestment cycles explicitly.  
- **Target weights:** Equal 20% each (simple, research-oriented; not optimized).  
- **Material-deterioration rule (conditional replacement policy):** Replace a holding only if (a) leadership is lost or share falls >10 percentage points from the observation used here in the defined market, or (b) ROIC falls sustainably below ~15% (or WACC + margin of safety) for multiple periods, or (c) FCF turns negative for two consecutive quarters without a clear temporary capex explanation, or (d) a material regulatory, techno

RESEARCH MEMO — P09: Four-Regime Rotation (recommendations-only evaluation)

Template ID: P09 — Four-Regime Rotation
Recommendation status: UNRESOLVED. The decision-driving inputs were recovered and verified from primary records, but both the growth leg and the inflation leg measure exactly zero change at published precision, and no prior regime state was supplied, so no rising/falling classification — and therefore no ETF allocation — can be prescribed without inventing a tie-break rule.
Evaluation month-end (decision time): 2026-09-30 at 16:00 America/New_York
Common research cutoff: 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z
Retrieval date (this research): 2026-10-16
Scope: dated research recommendation only. No orders placed or queued; no broker, trading venue, private account, or private connector accessed; no account state changed; no schedule deployed; no monitoring started. No local files read or written, no shell invoked, no tools installed, no non-public data used. Only public web search/fetch plus arithmetic and method exposition in this response.

1. Declared methodology (fixed before any computation)

Growth series: U.S. civilian unemployment rate, U-3, seasonally adjusted — BLS series LNS14000000 (FRED: UNRATE). Monthly, percent, as published to one decimal.
Inflation series: CPI-U, all items, 12-month percent change, not seasonally adjusted — BLS CUUR0000SA0 12-month change (FRED: CPIAUCSL-derived). Monthly, percent, as published to one decimal.
Release/lag policy: at each month-end, use the latest reference month whose scheduled BLS release occurred on or before that month-end at 16:00 ET. For 2026-09-30 this means August 2026 references for both series. The September 2026 Employment Situation (released 2026-10-02) and September 2026 CPI (released 2026-10-14) exist by the research cutoff but are excluded by this frozen vintage rule.
Transformation: none — use published values directly; comparison = latest published value minus the immediately preceding published value.
Sign rule (strict inequalities): growth is RISING iff UNRATE(latest) < UNRATE(prior); FALLING iff greater. Inflation is RISING iff CPI12m(latest) > CPI12m(prior); FALLING iff lower.
Zero-change rule: a
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 7466–9268
ly when the resulting resolved regime differs from the previously resolved regime. Months that remain unresolved prescribe no allocation and assert no trade.

7. Limitations, coverage, unresolved items

This is a verified absence of a determinate sign, not missing data: all four decision-driving values (4.1%, 4.1%, 3.4%, 3.4%) were recovered and agree across sources. The comparison basis is one-decimal published precision, deliberately chosen for reproducibility; unrounded internal BLS values could differ, but the convention does not use them. Geography and horizon

Evidence freshness
    Prefer records published within 180 days before the cutoff. Older records establish mechanisms and historical experience, not proof that an earlier shortage remains unresolved.

Meaning of binding constraint
    The input whose shortage most delays energised, usable AI capacity on a project’s critical path. Demand growth, order growth and market size alone do not qualify.

    The conceptual test is:
        Commissioning date = latest readiness date among compute, memory,
        networking, cooling and electrical power.

    A national conclusion ideally requires project-level, capacity-weighted readiness dates. Those data were not obtained, so the result is qualitative rather than a computed national bottleneck ranking.

Direct economic exposure
    First preference: disclosed AI/data-center revenue or orders attributable to products or services that relieve the constraint.
    Second preference: relevant segment revenue divided by consolidated revenue, explicitly identified as an imperfect proxy.
    Backlog is supporting evidence, not equivalent to revenue, cash flow or an independently verified shortage.

1. Fixed methodology and conventions

These are delegated research choices, not a claim that the original strategy specified these particular economic proxies. They remain fixed for this trial.

Growth series:
  BLS CES0000000001 — all employees, total nonfarm, seasonally adjusted, in thousands. Payroll momentum is the chosen proxy for U.S. growth, not a direct measure of real GDP. The BLS series metadata confirms the identifier, coverage and units. Current numerical observations on that metadata page are not used as historical inputs. ([data.bls.gov](https://data.bls.gov/timeseries/CES0000000001))

Inflation series:
  BLS CUSR0000SA0 — CPI-U, all items, U.S. city average, seasonally adjusted, base 1982–84=100. The signal uses the officially published monthly percentage changes associated with this series, at their published one-decimal precision. It does not use core CPI or year-over-year inflation. ([data.bls.gov](https://data.bls.gov/timeseries/CUSR0000SA0))

Transformations:
  Let E(m,v) denote payroll employment in observation month m and release vintage v.
  Monthly payroll gain: J(m,v) = E(m,v) − E(m−1,v).
  Growth acceleration: G = J(m,v) − J(m−1,v).

  Let P(m,v) denote the CPI index.
  Monthly inflation rate: R(m,v) = the BLS-published, one-decimal monthly percentage change corresponding to 100 × [P(m,v)/P(m−1,v) − 1].
  Inflation acceleration: I = R(m,v) − R(m−1,v).

Comparison window:
  Compare the latest eligible observation month with its immediately preceding

rice analysis or claim of statistical significance is supplied. Those are outside this reconstruction.

5. Complete prescribed allocation map

As supplied in the mandate:

  Growth rising / inflation falling:
    100% SPY.

  Growth falling / inflation falling:
    100% TLT.

  Growth rising / inflation rising:
    50% GLD plus 50% DBC.

  Growth falling / inflation rising:
    50% GLD plus 50% SGOV.

6. Conditional future review/change policy

At a future month-end review, if separately requested:

  Reapply the same identifiers, transformations, release rules and sign rules using only information available by that month-end’s 16:00 ET evaluation time.

  If both signs are resolved and the regime differs from the last established monthly regime, recommend the newly mapped allocation.

  If the regime is unchanged, recommend no regime-driven change. Do not recommend monthly rebalancing solely to restore weights after market drift.

  If required evidence is missing or a zero-change signal cannot be initialized, report the new regime as unresolved and recommend no new regime-driven switch. This is not evidence that the prior regime remains economically correct.

  Preserve each month’s original inputs and release vintage in the research record; incorporate later revisions only in subsequent evaluations.

For this initial recommendation, no prior portfolio or regime is supplied. Accordingly, the result is a 50% GLD / 50% DBC initial research target, with no assertion that a change from another allocation is required.

3. **Market-cap vendor conventions** (fully diluted vs. basic shares outstanding, treatment of dual-class totals) differ by ≲1% across sources; none of the retrieved differences approach the 5.1% leader gap.
4. **Stale context rows:** Alphabet/Microsoft figures are 2026-10-14 vintage (E8, E9) and were not used in the #1 determination; a same-close refresh would not plausibly close a >$1T gap.

## 8. Summary recommendation and its sensitivity

- **Primary recommendation:** **50% GLD + 50% SGOV** (growth falling, inflation rising, full-precision convention).
- **Sensitivity (disclosed, not tuned):** if the inflation sign is instead read from BLS's 1-decimal published prints (3.4% vs 3.4%, a tie → falling), the regime is growth falling + inflation falling and the allocation would be **100% TLT**. The full-precision index computation (+0.0317 pp) is the declared primary because it uses BLS's actual index levels rather than display rounding; the tie is nonetheless a genuine, material fork.
- **Growth sign is robust** across all available vintages: falling.
- **Unresolved:** none for the growth component; the inflation component is resolved only under the declared precision convention (exact unresolved input = whether "change" is measured at 3-decimal index precision or 1-decimal headline precision).

# P01: S&P1 — dated research recommendation

**Memo date:** 2026-10-15  
**Decision reference time:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York regular-session close  
**Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z  
**Recommendation status:** **Evidence-supported recommendation; no execution**

## Decision

- **Company:** NVIDIA Corporation  
- **Ticker:** **NVDA**  
- **Target allocation from hypothetical cash:** **100% NVDA**
- **Hypothetical cash allocation:** 0%
- **Estimated company-level market capitalization at the reference close:** **approximately $5.25 trillion**

Public market-data sources identify NVIDIA as the largest company by market capitalization at the October 15 close. The closest credible contender, Apple, was approximately $4.99 trillion. The rounded lead was therefore approximately **$0.26 trillion**, or **5.2%** over Apple.

No broker, account, trading venue, private connector, schedule, monitor, or order was accessed or used. No order or account change occurred.

## Methodology and conventions

1. Eligible companies: S&P 500 constituents evidenced as of the decision period.
2. Valuation measure: total company-level equity market capitalization, not index weight or share price alone.
3. Share classes: consolidated where the data provider reports a company-level figure. Alphabet’s Class A and Class C shares are treated as one company.
4. Valuation time: regular U.S. market close on October 15, 2026, 16:00 EDT.
5. Tie rule: lexicographic company-name order, as specified. No tie occurred.
6. Ranking approach: direct comparison of the leader with the nearest credible mega-cap contenders, supported by market-cap ranking pages and close-date market data.
7. Rounding: figures are reported to two decimal places in trillions. Differences calculated from rounded figures are approximate.
8. Source hierarchy: S&P Dow Jones Indices for index membership; public secondary market-data providers for close prices and market capitalization. No primary exchange market-cap file or issuer share-count record at the exact close was retrieved.

### Ranking arithmetic

Using the close-date figures:

- NVIDIA minus Apple: `$5.25T − $4.99T ≈ $0.26T`
- NVIDIA lead over Apple: `$0.26T ÷ $4.99T ≈ 5.2%`
- NVIDIA minus Alphabet: approximately `$0.98T`
- NVIDIA minus Microsoft: approximately `$1.24T`

The lead is material relative to the rounding precision of the reported figures.

The requested 10-stock, 10%-each recommendation **cannot be established from the verified evidence gathered within the research budget**.

The declared roster contains 20 distinct SEC 13F filers. However, only 10 managers have sufficiently extracted eligible top-holding data for a reproducible partial count.   4. **TSM — Taiwan Semiconductor ADR**
  5. **V — Visa**
- These names are **provisional observations only**, not a recommendation or current-position instruction.

## Declared methodology

### Roster convention

I selected 20 distinct long-horizon or fundamental/activist public-equity managers using this fixed convention:

1. The filer must be a distinct SEC 13F reporting adviser or investment entity.
2. The manager must have public evidence of a fundamental, concentrated, activist, long-only, value, growth-equity, or explicitly long-term investment approach.
3. Affiliated entities are deduplicated by adviser identity and SEC CIK.
4. A filing’s “other included managers” are not counted as separate managers.
5. The roster is fixed before the consensus count; it is not adjusted to favor particular stocks.

This is a defensible convention, not the only possible roster. Appalo

## Conditional future review and change policy

This strategy should be reviewed **only after a new common completed 13F filing cycle**.

At each review:

1. Preserve the declared 20-manager roster unless a manager ceases to qualify as a distinct filer or no longer has a valid 13F.
2. Collect the latest valid HR/HR/A for the common quarter.
3. Reapply the same stock, ADR, option, ETF, issuer-aggregation, and ticker-mapping rules.
4. Require all 20 managers to have supported filings and complete eligible top-10 extraction.
5. Count each issuer once per manager.
6. Rank by consensus count, then the declared deterministic tie rule.
7. Only if the complete ranking is established, target 10% for each of the ten selected issuers.
8. Do not change the basket between filing cycles because of price moves, news, or inferred trades.
9. If fewer than 20 managers or any material mapping/version conflict remains unresolved, retain an **unresolved/provisional** status rather than treating a partial sample as the basket.

If current holdings are later supplied, the conditional rule is: hold names that remain in the next verified basket at their target weight; reduce or remove names that leave it; add names entering it, subject to separate account authorization. No current positions were assumed here.

# P03 — Congressional Conviction Research Memo

**Template ID:** P03  
**Recommendation status:** **UNRESOLVED / PROVISIONAL NO-ALLOCATION**  
**Decision time:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z  
**Reference close:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York  
**Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z  

No qualifying security is verified from the accessible public evidence. The result is **not evidence that no security qualifies**. The House and Senate disclosure corpus could not be exhaustively validated through the permitted public-web interface, and date-only secondary records create unresolved availability-date conflicts. Therefore, no new allocation is recommended pending a verified trigger.

No orders, account changes, schedules, monitors, broker connections, or other execution actions were performed.

## 1. Frozen methodology

### Entry window

The required interval is:

\[
(2026\text{-}09\text{-}16T03{:}15Z,\;2026\text{-}10\text{-}16T03{:}15Z]
\]

The actionable clock is public disclosure availability, not the original transaction date.

Most retrieved records expose only a calendar filing date, not a publication timestamp. For date-only records, I used the conservative operational convention:

- include filings dated **2026-09-17 through 2026-10-15**;
- exclude 2026-09-16 and 2026-10-16 records unless a timestamp establishes inclusion;
- treat filing-date conflicts as unresolved, not silently corrected.

### Security universe

Included: U.S.-listed common stock identified by a ticker or exact share class.  
Excluded from the stock screen: bonds, municipal securities, structured products, private LLC interests, options, and executive-branch disclosures.

`GOOG` and `GOOGL` are kept distinct because the official record identifies the security class.

### Qualification

A security qualifies only if:

1. purchases are attributed to at least **three distinct members of Congress**;
2. the sum of disclosed **lower bounds** is at least **$500,000**;
3. each counted purchase falls in the public-availability window.

A member counts once even if multiple accounts or multiple purchases are reported. Spouse/dependent transactions count under the reporting member only, with ow
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 12996–14796
 cluster pages are not sufficient by themselves.

5. **Range data are not exact amounts.** The lower-bound test is applied only to the left edge of each disclosed range. Midpoints and upper bounds are not used to confirm qualification.

Because these gaps leave the screen unresolved, this memo does not rank securities or label any name as a qualifying pick.

## 7. Conditional recommendation policy

Maintain **no new allocation pending a verified trigger**.

A future review should add a security only when a complete, corrected public ledger confirms:

- at least three distinct congressional members;
- purchases publicly available within the then-current 30-calendar-day window;
- lower-bound total at least $500,000;
- U.S.-listed stock identity and ticker normalization;
- no exclusion under the stock-universe convention.

If more than five names qualify, use the fixed ranking rules stated above and equal-weight the selected names.

### Conditional exit rule

For a verified entry:

- the holding clock starts when the threshold first becomes publicly confirmable;
- the intended holding period is six calendar months;
- if no anniversary date exists, use the last calendar day of the sixth month;
- exit earlier when any member whose purchase helped qualify the entry publicly discloses a sale of that security.

No incumbent holdings were supplied. Accordingly, no current exit is asserted.

### Conditional performance clock

Any performance measurement begins at actionable public disclosure availability, not at the original transaction date. The report should use the disclosed price or a documented closing-price convention at the actionable timestamp, with the convention recorded for each entry.

No performance calculation was made because no verified P03 entry was established.

The public product page establishes that an appropriate historical dataset exists or is offered. It does **not** provide the required constituent-level September 2026 records in this research trace.

6. If fewer than five qualify, do not backfill with nonqualifiers.
7. If required historical inputs are unavailable, label the result unresolved rather than substituting current consensus, narrative views, or a price-only ranking.

# P06 — Oligopoly Champions

**Recommendation date:** 2026-10-16  
**Decision cutoff:** 2026-10-16 03:15:00Z  
**Reference market close:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York  
**Status:** **Supported conditional research recommendation; three qualifiers, no padding to five names.**  
**Research basis:** Public web sources only. Records were retrieved during this research run; publication or filing dates below establish availability. No post-cutoff evidence was used.

## Recommendation

| Target | Identifier | Defined concentrated market | Target weight | Status |
|---|---|---|---:|---|
| ASML Holding N.V. | ASML / ASML.AS | Worldwide commercial EUV lithography-system manufacturing, 2025 | **40%** | Qualifies |
| Visa Inc. | V | Global six-network card purchase-volume market, 2024 | **35%** | Qualifies |
| S&P Global Inc. | SPGI | EU credit-rating services by revenue, 2024 | **25%** | Qualifies |

Weights express business-quality and leadership conviction, not valuation. No price or valuation screen was applied; the evidence does not establish that any name was inexpensive at the reference close.

---

## Methodology and fixed conventions

1. **Market admission:**  
   - HHI of at least 2,500, using percentage-point shares, **or**
   - CR3 of at least 70%.
2. **Leadership:** The selected company must be the largest participant within the same defined market and observation period used for concentration.
3. **ROIC:**  
   \[
   \text{ROIC}=\frac{\text{EBIT}\times(1-\text{effective tax rate})}
   {\text{average invested capital}}
   \]
   \[
   \text{Invested capital}=\text{total debt}+\text{total equity}-\text{cash and cash equivalents}
   \]
   Effective tax rate is reported income-tax provision divided by pretax income.
4. **Free cash flow:**  
   \[
   \text{FCF}=\text{cash flow from operations}-\text{capital expenditures}
   \]
   Capital expenditures include PP&E and intangible purchases where disclosed. S&P’s normalized calculation excludes distributions to noncontrolling interests; its separately reported company FCF is also shown.
5. **Quality period:** Latest completed fiscal year available by the cutoff, generally FY2025.
6. **Weight convention:** Higher 
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 16093–17893
 longer the sole commercial EUV supplier; or
- Visa loses the largest position in the defined six-network purchase-volume market, with a competitor ahead; or
- S&P loses the largest EU CRA revenue share, with a competitor ahead; or
- Defined-market share falls by at least 5 percentage points and the company is no longer the leader.

**Financial/business-quality breach**

- ROIC falls below 15% for two consecutive reporting periods, excluding a clearly disclosed one-time accounting or litigation effect; or
- FCF becomes negative for two consecutive periods; or
- FCF margin falls below 20% for two consecutive periods without a credible temporary explanation; or
- Operating margin falls by at least 500 basis points for two consecutive periods and management attributes the decline to persistent competition, lost pricing power, or structural cost pressure.

**Permanent structural event**

- A credible technological substitute displaces the defined market.
- Regulation removes a material part of the franchise economics.
- Reputational, legal, or operational damage permanently impairs customer acceptance.
- Debt-funded acquisition or capital allocation materially weakens the quality profile.

Do **not** replace solely because of price volatility, a short-term earnings miss, one weak quarter, temporary issuance-cycle softness at S&P, or a valuation change. A replacement must independently satisfy the same market-definition, concentration, leadership, ROIC, and FCF tests. If evidence becomes unavailable, mark the name unresolved and seek updated evidence; do not invent a market-share result.

**Execution status:** No orders were placed or queued. No broker, trading venue, private account, private connector, account state, schedule, or monitoring process was accessed or changed.

## Methodology and fixed conventions

1. **Market admission:**  
   - HHI of at least 2,500, using percentage-point shares, **or**
   - CR3 of at least 70%.
2. **Leadership:** The selected company must be the largest participant within the same defined market and observation period used for concentration.
3. **ROIC:**  
   \[
   \text{ROIC}=\frac{\text{EBIT}\times(1-\text{effective tax rate})}
   {\text{average invested capital}}
   \]
   \[
   \text{Invested capital}=\text{total debt}+\text{total equity}-\text{cash and cash equivalents}
   \]
   Effective tax rate is reported income-tax provision divided by pretax income.
4. **Free cash flow:**  
   \[
   \text{FCF}=\text{cash flow from operations}-\text{capital expenditures}
   \]
   Capital expenditures include PP&E and intangible purchases where disclosed. S&P’s normalized calculation excludes distributions to noncontrolling interests; its separately reported company FCF is also shown.
5. **Quality period:** Latest completed fiscal year available by the cutoff, generally FY2025.
6. **Weight convention:** Higher weights go to stronger concentration, clearer leadership, higher ROIC, stronger FCF conversion, and greater confidence in durability. No valuation adjustment.
7. **Replacement rule:** No replacement based solely on share-price movement, one weak quarter, or valuation.

Visa is the largest network by purchase volume, narrowly ahead of UnionPay and well ahead of Mastercard.

**Pricing power:** Visa’s FY2025 Form 10-K describes a network of nearly 5 billion payment credentials, approximately 14,500 financial institutions, and more than 175 million merchant locations. It also states that revenue benefited from “select pricing modifications.” The network effect, acceptance footprint, reliability, security, and cross-border capability support value-based pricing. Pricing power is constrained by client incentives, regulation, domestic payment systems, and real-time/account-to-account alternatives.

**Source:** [Visa FY2025 Form 10-K](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1403161/000140316125000089/v-20250930.htm), filed November 6, 2025.

# P07: AI Bottleneck — Monthly Research Recommendation

**Recommendation date:** 2026-10-16  
**Template ID:** P07  
**Recommendation status:** **PROVISIONAL QUALIFIED BASKET**  
**Relevant decision time:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z  
**Reference regular U.S. market close:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York  
**Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z  

All evidence below was published or otherwise available no later than the cutoff, subject to the source-date limitations noted in the ledger. Public web search only. No broker, account, private connector, local file, shell, order, schedule, or monitoring system was accessed or changed.

## 1. Conventions

- **Geography:** U.S. AI data-center expansion, with global semiconductor and equipment supply chains.
- **Planning horizon:** The next 12–24 months, emphasizing capacity that can be commissioned during 2026–2027.
- **Binding constraint:** The physical or regulatory constraint that most restricts converting additional AI compute demand into commissioned, reliable operating capacity. High demand or high prices alone do not qualify.
- **Direct economic exposure:** Evidence that a company sells the constrained equipment or infrastructure, measured using disclosed data-center orders, revenue, backlog, product position, or customer demand. Market popularity is excluded.
- **FCF horizon:** Latest company FY2026 free-cash-flow guidance available by the cutoff, supplemented by reported 2026 cash generation.
- **FCF comparability:** Limited. GE Vernova reports company-wide non-GAAP FCF; Vertiv reports adjusted FCF; Eaton reports FCF. No segment-level FCF is disclosed consistently.
- **Allocation rule:** Provisional weights reflect a qualitative combination of direct exposure to the selected bottleneck and supported FCF outlook. This is not a valuation, risk-parity, or FCF-yield allocation. No current holdings were supplied, so “hold” and “change” rules are conditional.

## 2. Conclusion

### Binding bottleneck

**The best-supported bottleneck is usable, reliable power delivery to AI facilities: interconnection, transmission and grid-supporting equipment such as transformers, UPS systems, switchgear and substations.**

Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 18880–20690
ming anchor.

## 7. Conditional review and rotation policy

Review monthly using the same conventions and cutoff discipline.

### Maintain the provisional basket if

- FERC, DOE, NERC, utility filings or credible independent studies continue to show large-load interconnection, transmission or grid-supporting equipment as the main commissioning delay;
- Eaton, GE Vernova and Vertiv maintain or improve relevant order/backlog evidence; and
- FY2026 or subsequent FCF guidance remains intact without evidence that cash generation is primarily non-recurring.

### Consider changing the bottleneck and roster only if new evidence shows

- Semiconductor or HBM shortages cause more widespread or longer AI-capacity delays than power interconnection/equipment;
- Transmission and equipment lead times materially normalize while chip, memory, networking or cooling lead times worsen;
- A company’s relevant exposure falls, orders deteriorate materially, or FCF guidance is cut for reasons tied to the selected bottleneck; or
- A new public company provides stronger direct exposure and more reliable FCF evidence under the same declared methodology.

Conditional alternatives:

- **Compute:** NVIDIA, if supply allocation rather than site power becomes the demonstrated marginal constraint.
- **Memory:** Micron, if HBM supply remains the measured deployment limiter and its FCF outlook remains supported.
- **Networking:** Broadcom, if network silicon or advanced packaging shows quantified project delays.
- **Cooling:** Vertiv, already included, would gain weight if liquid-cooling capacity becomes the measured constraint.

With no incumbent holdings supplied, no actual “sell,” “buy,” or rotation action is recommended or implied. **No orders, account changes, schedules or monitors were executed.**

# P09: Four-Regime Rotation — dated research memo

**Recommendation status:** Supported initial research recommendation  
**Evaluation time:** 2026-09-30 16:00 America/New_York  
**Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z  
**Reference regular close:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York  
**Research access:** Public web sources only. No broker, account, connector, order, schedule, or monitor was accessed or executed.

## 1. Declared methodology

### Series

- **Growth proxy:** `INDPRO`, Federal Reserve, Industrial Production: Total Index.
  - Monthly, seasonally adjusted, index 2017=100.
  - ALFRED: <https://alfred.stlouisfed.org/series?seid=INDPRO>
- **Inflation proxy:** `CPIAUCSL`, BLS CPI-U All Items, U.S. City Average.
  - Monthly, seasonally adjusted, index 1982–1984=100.
  - ALFRED: <https://alfred.stlouisfed.org/series?seid=CPIAUCSL>

These are revision-aware series. Inputs are taken from the latest release vintage available by the evaluation time, not from observations released afterward.

### Release and lag policy

At each month-end, select the latest observation whose source release was publicly available by 16:00 America/New_York on that month-end. For 2026-09-30:

- Latest eligible `INDPRO` observation: **August 2026**, released September 18.
- Latest eligible `CPIAUCSL` observation: **August 2026**, released September 11.
- September observations are excluded because they were not available at the September 30 evaluation time.

### Transformations and comparison windows

For each series \(X_t\), calculate the one-month percentage change:

\[
r_t = 100\left(\frac{X_t}{X_{t-1}}-1\right)
\]

Classify the indicator by comparing the latest one-month rate with the prior one-month rate:

- **Rising:** \(r_t > r_{t-1}\)
- **Falling:** \(r_t < r_{t-1}\)
- **Zero/indeterminate:** \(r_t = r_{t-1}\) at the source precision

This requires three consecutive monthly observations: June, July, and August 2026. No annualization is applied.

The sign comparison uses the unrounded source index values. A release narrative rounded to `0.0%` does not override a positive difference in the underlying index values.

### Missing-data and initialization rules

- If any r

## 5. Conditional future policy

- Recompute the same indicators at each month-end using the same series, transformations, release-vintage rule, and zero-change rule.
- Change the prescribed allocation only when the newly classified regime differs from the last recorded regime.
- If the regime is unchanged, retain the prior prescribed allocation conditionally.
- If required data are missing, unrecoverable, or tied, leave the regime and allocation unresolved rather than inventing a classification.
- The first computable classification above is the initialization result because no prior regime was supplied.
- No order was placed or queued; no account state was changed; no schedule or ongoing monitoring was deployed.

## 6. Material uncertainties

1. **Delegated convention:** The mandate did not specify the exact series or transformation. This memo fixes `INDPRO`, `CPIAUCSL`, one-month percentage changes, and month-over-month rate comparisons before presenting the result. A different defensible convention could produce a different regime.
2. **Small growth-rate change:** August industrial production increased only about 0.0221% from July, while July increased about 0.1978% from June. The growth classification is therefore falling because momentum decelerated, not because the production index declined.
3. **Rounding distinction:** The Federal Reserve’s release rounded the August monthly IP change to 0.0%; the calculation uses the underlying ALFRED index values.
4. **Seasonal-adjustment distinction:** The selected `CPIAUCSL` value is seasonally adjusted. It must not be mixed with the separate non-seasonally-adjusted CPI-U index reported in some BLS tables.

# P11 — Google PageRank Research Recommendation

**Recommendation status:** **Unresolved; no qualifying top-10 recommendation.**  
**Template:** P11 — Google PageRank  
**Decision time:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York reference market close  
**Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16 03:15:00Z  
**Retrieval context:** Public web search only, retrieved on 2026-10-16. No broker, private account, connector, local file, order, schedule, or monitor was accessed or created.

The required S&P 500 earnings-mention graph was not established end-to-end. A top-10 list, equal-weight allocation, PageRank scores, or ranking claim would therefore be unsupported.

## 1. Decision

**Do not issue a new P11 top-10 selection or rebalance recommendation from this trial.**

Reason: the following required artifacts are incomplete:

- Exact frozen S&P 500 constituent-security snapshot effective at the reference close.
- Complete first-party earnings-release, prepared-remarks, and company-published-transcript corpus for every eligible constituent in the selected cycle.
- Complete mention-to-ticker mapping, including exclusions and ambiguity records.
- Complete deduplicated directed edge list, including every isolate and dangling node.
- Actual PageRank calculation, convergence diagnostics, and score vector.

This is **insufficient evidence**, not verified evidence that the graph contains no useful signal.

## 2. Frozen conventions

These conventions were selected before presenting any ranking. No ranking was produced.

- **Share classes:** Each publicly listed eligible share class is a separate node when S&P DJI treats it as a separate constituent security. The canonical node identifier is the U.S.-listed ticker/share-class line.

1. Freeze the date-specific S&P 500 security-line universe at the new decision close.

A future recommendation changes only after a new completed earnings cycle and a successful full rerun. No orders, account changes, schedules, monitoring processes, or other ongoing actions were executed.

I am not substituting current consensus, analyst commentary, or momentum alone for those inputs.

### Predeclared reconstruction conventions

| Decision | Fixed method |
|---|---|
| Universe | Use companies in the S&P 500 effective at the decision time, not current constituents. Consolidate multiple share classes at the company level and retain the class designated by the effective-dated index roster. |
| Consensus and forecast | Use Zacks historical EPS consensus as documented by Nasdaq Data Link. Compare the same first not-yet-ended annual fiscal forecast period at both endpoints. |
| Revision endpoints | Use a 30-calendar-day lookback. At each endpoint, select the latest observation whose publication by the decision time can be established. Do not assume an observation dated at month-end was available at that market close. |
| Upward strength | Change in mean EPS consensus divided by the absolute starting mean. Qualification requires a positive starting mean and a positive change. |
| Breadth | For analysts identifiable at both endpoints for that fiscal period, subtract the fraction whose estimates fell from the fraction whose estimates rose. Qualification requires more increases than decreases. Do not treat provider aggregate up/down counts as distinct analysts across the lookback without evidence that they have that meaning. |
| Momentum | Use the change in split- and dividend-adjusted regular-session closing price over six calendar months, ending at the latest close verifiably available by the decision time. Use the prece
