| Effective-dated index membership | Required at the September decision time; roster not retrieved. | Fetching the [S&P Dow Jones Indices S&P 500 page](https://www.spglobal.com/spdji/en/indices/equity/sp-500/) returned **Access Denied**, not a constituent record. | Eligible companies and coverage could not be verified. **P03: Congressional Conviction. Research memo, dated 2026-10-16**

**Status: no confirmed trigger, with a coverage gap (unresolved, not a verified absence).** I recommend no new allocation and holding cash until a trigger is verified. The decision time is the common research cutoff, 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z. The reference close is 2026-10-15 16:00 ET, and the time zone is America/New_York. The filings were retrieved on 2026-10-16 at about 19:01Z, after the cutoff, but only filings dated before the cutoff were used.

**Conventions (set before looking at results)**
- **Window:** the 30 days (2026-09-16T03:15Z, 2026-10-16T03:15Z], keyed to when each filing became public. The House and Senate indexes only give a filing date, not a time, so I used filing dates 9/16 through 10/16. No filing in the window falls on either boundary day (House filings run 9/17 to 10/15, Senate 9/17 to 10/07), so the time-of-day issue doesn't affect the result.
- **What counts:** purchases of U.S.-listed stocks, including partial purchases. Assets tagged [ST] or with no tag were counted; bonds, Treasuries, funds and LLCs were excluded.
- **Who counts:** a spouse's (SP), joint (JT) or dependent's (DC) purchase counts under the member who filed it, and each member counts once.
- **Thresholds:** at least 3 distinct members, and the disclosed range lower bounds must add up to at least $500,000. A ticker that only clears $500,000 on upper bounds would be listed separately as unconfirmed.
- **Tie rule if more than five qualify:** rank by lower-bound total (highest first), then by member count (highest first), then by ticker alphabetically. Take the top five at equal weight.

**Coverage**
- **House:** the [2026 House disclosure index](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2026FD.zip) lists 37 PTRs (periodic transaction reports) filed in the window. I parsed 30 electronic ones, which gave 324 transaction rows, 121 of them purchases.
- **House gap:** 7 are scanned paper filings with no text layer, and I couldn't read them:
  - Harshbarger: 9116355, 9116356, 9116357 (filed 10/1)
  - Khanna: 9116376 (10/7)
  - McCaul: 9116378 (10/14)
  - Rogers: 9116342 (9/23)
  - Wied: 9116361 
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 4280–6080
ome entries in Cisneros's long PDF are cut off at line breaks.
- The Senate WFC row was merged into the totals by hand.
- Given how far every ticker is from the thresholds, these issues don't change the result.

**Conditional policy going forward**
- **Entry:** a ticker becomes a buy when a filing first pushes it to 3 or more distinct members and $500,000 or more in lower-bound purchases within the 30-day window. That filing's public date starts both the holding clock and the performance clock.
- **Weights:** equal weight across at most five qualifying names, using the tie rule above.
- **Exit:** six calendar months after entry. If that month has no matching date, exit on its last day. Exit earlier if any member whose purchase helped qualify the name publicly discloses a sale of it.
- **If no name qualifies:** stay in cash.
- **Note on interpretation:** this exit rule is the fixed interpretation for this trial. The original mandate's "hold six months or until a disclosed sale" left the details open.
- No holdings were supplied, so there are no current positions to hold or exit.

**Supporting files**
They're all in `/workspace/grok-bot-native-P03-trial-1-20261016`, a new folder; nothing already on my computer was opened.

- `house_ptr_window_index.tsv`: the 37 House PTRs in the window

# P04 Insider Cluster: research memo (recommendations only)

**Template ID:** P04 Insider Cluster
**Status:** No S&P 500 company qualifies, so I'm recommending no names. Within the coverage below, this is a checked absence rather than a lack of evidence. The remaining risks are in section 6.
**Decision time (reference close):** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York
**Research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z, which is 2026-10-15 23:15 ET
**Cluster window (transaction dates):** 2026-09-16 to 2026-10-15
**When I retrieved the data:** 2026-10-16, about 19:00 to 19:15 UTC. Every filing I relied on was accepted by the SEC before the cutoff. I opened later filings only to rule them out.

## 1. Recommendation
I recommend no names and no weights, which means staying in cash under this strategy. I fixed these rules before looking at any results:
- **If more than five companies qualify:** rank by total dollar value of qualifying purchases, highest first. Break ties by the earliest SEC acceptance time of the filing that completes the cluster, then by ticker A to Z.
- **Weights:** equal, so each of N selected names gets 1/N (20% each if there are five).
- **Exits:** six months after SEC acceptance of the filing that completes the cluster. Exit earlier if any qualifying insider publicly discloses an open-market sale (transaction code S) that wasn't made under a trading plan.

## 2. Method and conventions (fixed in advance)
1. **Index membership:** I used Wikipedia's "List of S&P 500 companies", the [revision from 2026-10-14 03:35 UTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=List_of_S%26P_500_companies&oldid=1380159481) (oldid 1380159481). It was the last revision before the reference close and lists 503 tickers with their SEC company IDs (CIKs). This is a secondary source, and I didn't check it against S&P Dow Jones Indices.
2. **Filings:** I used the SEC EDGAR daily form indexes for 2026-09-16 to 2026-10-15 (`https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/daily-index/2026/QTR3/` and `/QTR4/`, files `form.YYYYMMDD.idx`). They held 21,909 Form 4 and 4/A rows. Of those, 2,073 filings came from companies on the S&P list, and I downloaded all 2,073 with no failures. The index for 2026-10-12 re
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 7377–9177
discloses an open-market sale not made under a plan.

## 6. Coverage gaps and limitations
- **Membership:** the list comes from Wikipedia, not S&P Dow Jones Indices. I matched companies to tickers by CIK, which also handles companies with two share classes because both classes share one CIK.
- **Form types and timing:** I didn't screen Form 5. A trade dated in the window but not filed by the cutoff is out of scope by design.
- **Roles:** roles come from each filing's relationship fields. I didn't cross-check them against proxy statements.
- **Earlier filings:** I didn't check Form 4s filed before 9/16. A trade dated on or after 9/16 can't appear in a filing made before 9/16, so this only matters if a filing was back-dated.
- **Plan classification:** I relied on the footnotes and the plan flag and didn't read the plan documents.

No orders, account changes, schedules or monitors were executed, and no broker or private data was used.

- **Index membership:** `s2_sp.py` saves the Wikipedia revision as `sp500_wiki_rev1380159481.html` and the parsed list as `sp500_members.csv`.

# P05: Revision Leaders. Research memo (recommendations only)

| Item | Value |

| Case folder | `/workspace/grok-bot-native-P05-trial-1-20261016`, created new for this run |

## 1. Rules I set before ranking (fixed for this trial)
- **Estimate source:** the Yahoo Finance "Analysis" page, which shows LSEG/Refinitiv consensus. Only archived copies captured on or before the decision time count.
- **Forecast period:** the current fiscal year's EPS consensus, which Yahoo labels "Current Year".
- **30-day window:** I compare the consensus on the 2026-09-30 capture with the "30 Days Ago" figure on that same page. A 2026-08-31 capture is only a cross-check. A capture up to 2 trading days before 9/30 is accepted but flagged, and nothing after 9/30 is used.
- **Strength:** (current-year EPS consensus now ÷ the same consensus 30 days earlier) − 1.
- **Breadth:** (upward revisions in the last 30 days − downward revisions) ÷ number of estimates.
- **Momentum:** total-return-adjusted price change from the 2026-03-31 close to the 2026-09-30 close, with no skipped month.
- **Normalization:** cross-sectional z-scores, capped at ±3.
- **Composite score:** 25% strength + 25% breadth + 50% momentum.
- **Qualification:** complete data, strength above 0 and breadth above 0. Companies with missing data are excluded.
- **Ties:** higher breadth wins, then alphabetical ticker.
- **Fewer than five qualify:** recommend only the companies that qualify, with no fill-ins. A partial sample is never presented as a universe-wide top five.
- **Universe:** S&P 500 members as of 2026-09-30. I didn't establish this list.

## 2. Coverage
- Revision data verifi

2. S&P 500 membership as of 2026-09-30.
3. Total-return-adjusted closing prices for 2026-03-31 and 2026-09-30.
4. The contents of the 9/28 NVDA archive. A longer timeout might load it, but one company can't support a universe ranking.

## 6. Monthly review policy (conditional only)
At each month-end close, rebuild the ranking from data available at that time.
- Keep a name that is still in the top five and still qualifies.
- Replace a name that drops out of the top five or stops qualifying with the next-ranked qualifier, then reset everything to equal weights.
- If fewer than five qualify, hold only the ones that qualify.
- If the data are insufficient, as they are this month, mark the month unresolved and recommend no change.

**P06 Oligopoly Champions: research memo (recommendation only)**

- **Template:** P06.
- **Status:** Partial. Two names are supported, S&P Global fully and Visa with a secondary-source caveat. I didn't test the other candidates, so their absence isn't a negative finding.
- **Decision time:** reference US close, 2026-10-15 16:00 ET (America/New_York).
- **Research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z.
- **When I pulled the data:** 2026-10-16 around 19:07 UTC, which is after the cutoff. I only used records published before the cutoff.

**1. Rules set before looking at results (kept fixed for the whole trial)**

**Markets**
- M1 is US credit ratings from SEC-registered rating agencies (NRSROs), measured by ratings outstanding in all categories at 2025-12-31.
- M2 is US general-purpose credit card purchase volume across Visa, Mastercard, Amex and Discover in calendar 2025.

**How a market qualifies:** the top three firms hold at least 80%, or the HHI (Herfindahl-Hirschman Index, the sum of squared market shares) is at least 2,500. Share data must cover the same market and period as the leadership claim. The leader is the firm with the largest share in that market.

**ROIC (return on invested capital) = NOPAT ÷ average invested capital**
- NOPAT is operating income × (1 − effective tax rate). The effective tax rate is income tax ÷ pre-tax income.
- Invested capital is total debt plus equity minus cash, averaged over the last two fiscal year-ends.
- Figures are consolidated, from the latest 10-K filed before the cutoff.

**FCF (free cash flow) = operating cash flow − capital spending**, also shown as FCF ÷ revenue and FCF ÷ net income.

**Quality bar:** ROIC of at least 10%, FCF ÷ net income of at least 0.9, and an FCF margin of at least 20%.

**Weights:** equal across names that qualify, at most five, and the list isn't padded.

**2. Recommendation (provisional; no positions are assumed)**

| Name | Weight | Market | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| S&P Global (SPGI, CIK 64040) | 50% | M1 | Primary SEC data |
| Visa (V, CIK 1403161) | 50% | M2 | Secondary share source (Nilson Report) |

**3. Markets, concentration and leadership**

**M1, credit ratings.** Ratings outstanding
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 6506–8307
**Leadership lost:** the company is no longer the largest by share in its defined market. For M1 this would show in the next SEC NRSRO data, and for M2 in the next Nilson annual figures.
- **Market no longer concentrated:** the HHI falls below 2,500 and the top-three share falls below 80%.
- **Quality failure:** on a trailing basis, ROIC falls below 10%, FCF ÷ net income falls below 0.8, or FCF margin falls below 20%.
- **Structural hit to pricing power:** a final regulatory or court action cuts the company's core fee economics. For Visa that could be a DOJ antitrust ruling or US interchange or routing legislation. For S&P it could be a change to NRSRO rules.

Otherwise, the recommendation is to hold.

**8. Limitations**

- Data was pulled after the cutoff. Every figure was filtered by its filing or publication date, and all 10-Ks were filed between Nov 2025 and Feb 2026. Visa's FY2026 10-K wasn't filed by the cutoff, so its figures are FY2025.
- ROIC is consolidated, not by segment.
- NRSRO shares count ratings, not revenue.
- The Nilson data is secondary, its publication date is unverified, and competitor shares are paywalled.
- I didn't use market prices, valuation or the 2026-10-15 close.

**9. Confirmation**

No orders, broker or account access, account changes, schedules or monitors were executed. I used only public data, in a new case folder, with no installs or background processes.

**Case artifacts** are in `/workspace/grok-bot-native-P06-trial-1-20261016/`:
- `roic_fcf.py` and `roic_fcf_results.csv` hold the ROIC and FCF math and outputs.
- `concentration.py` and `concentration_results.csv` hold the share and HHI math.
- `cf_0001403161.json`, `cf_0000064040.json`, `cf_0001059556.json` and `cf_0001141391.json` are the raw SEC company data files used as inputs.

# P07: AI Bottleneck. Research memo (recommendations only)

- **Template ID:** P07: AI Bottleneck
- **Recommendation status:** PROVISIONAL. I'm moderately confident in the bottleneck call. The basket is an equal-weight list with documented gaps (§6).
- **Research decision cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z. I used only material published by then.
- **Reference close:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York.
- **Retrieval:** all sources were retrieved 2026-10-16, about 19:10–19:25 UTC, which is after the cutoff. Anything dated after the cutoff was left out, including 2026-10-16 intraday prices.

## 1. Conventions (fixed for this trial)
| Convention | Choice |
|---|---|
| Geography | US deployment of AI data centers. Global supply chains count where they feed US builds. |
| Planning horizon | 2026-Q4 through year-end 2028 |
| Evidence freshness | Published 2026-04-01 to the cutoff. Older or undated items are flagged. |
| Binding constraint | The input whose limited supply actually delays or caps energized, operating AI capacity over the horizon. It needs evidence of delayed or rationed deployment, not just demand. Easing it would raise deployment more than easing any other category, and it can't be fixed within about 12 months. |
| Direct-exposure measure | Share of revenue from segments that sell equipment into the binding category, ranked from segment and management disclosures. I didn't extract exact percentages (gap). |
| FCF definition | Cash from operations minus capex, from SEC XBRL. TTM = FY2025 + H1-2026 − H1-2025. |
| FCF forecast horizon | FY2026, using management guidance only |
| Valuation | Market cap = XBRL cover-page shares × the 2026-10-15 close. FCF yield = FCF ÷ market cap. |
| Allocation rule | Equal weight, 1/3 each |

tional review and rotation policy (nothing scheduled)
At each monthly review I'd rotate only if new evidence shows the bottleneck has changed. No incumbent portfolio was supplied, so all of these are conditional.
- **Rotate toward chips, packaging or HBM** (for example TSMC, Micron or SK hynix) if any of these show up: verified reports of energized capacity starved for GPUs, TSMC saying packaging stays limiting into 2027, or HBM allocation extending further.
- **Trim power-equipment names** if any of these show up: GEV misses its target of at least 125 GW of gas backlog plus reservations by year-end 2026, transformer or breaker lead times fall for two straight quarters, large-load queue cancellations speed up, or hyperscalers cut capex guidance.
- **Company triggers:** an FY26 FCF guidance cut or a miss of more than 10% against the midpoint, book-to-bill below 1.0 for two quarters, or TTM FCF yield below 2% with no growth upgrade.
- **Upgrade from provisional** once exposure percentages and independent FCF estimates are verified.

- The time to fix it is longer than the planning horizon.
- Primary manufacturer data show backlogs that run for years against limited annual output.

# P08 Lobbying-to-Revenue: research memo (recommendations only)

**Template:** P08. **Status:** provisional. The universe coverage is incomplete, one candidate is unresolved, and the contract data was pulled after the cutoff.
**Decision time:** reference close 2026-10-15 16:00 ET. **Research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z.

**Case folder:** `/workspace/grok-bot-native-P08-trial-1-20261016-c`. The suggested name and the `-b` variant already existed, so I didn't open them. I used only the Python standard library and curl, and installed nothing.

## 1. Recommendation
- **Stellantis N.V. (STLA, NYSE) gets 100% of the allocation.** It's the only name in the top five that confirmed.
- I didn't allocate to the other four, and I didn't backfill from Fluor (#6) or anything lower.
  - Coupang, PG&E and NVIDIA failed to confirm under the declared rules.
  - CVS is unresolved, which is not the same as failing.
- This is a provisional research suggestion, not a verified final top five. Section 7 explains why.

## 2. Conventions (fixed before ranking)
- **Data source:** the Senate LDA API, second-quarter filings for 2025 and 2026. The API now redirects to `https://lda.gov/api/v1/filings/`.
- **Self-filers only:** the client must be the registrant itself, either by the `client_self_select` flag or by an exact name match.
- **Spending basis:** only the LD-2 `expenses` field.
  - Every LD-2 filing type counts, including amendments, but not registrations (RR/RA).
  - For each registrant and quarter I kept the latest filing posted by the cutoff (`dt_posted` at or before the cutoff).
- **Universe pulled:** filings that reported at least $500k in either year.
  - That came to 474 filings for 2025 and 437 for 2026, or 362 unique registrants.
  - When one year's filing was missing, I fetched it by registrant ID.
- **Minimum base (declared up front):** a company needs at least $500k in Q2 2025 to be ranked. The 58 below that are listed separately.
- **U.S.-listed test:** the company or its parent must trade on NYSE or Nasdaq, as common shares or ADSs.
  - I checked this by hand for the ra

## 7. Limitations
- **Verified:** the LDA figures and URLs, and the USAspending totals as they stood when I retrieved them.
- **Coverage gaps:**
  - The $500k screen leaves out smaller filers.
  - I checked listings by hand for the ranked names only.
  - Companies that lobby only through outside firms (income basis) aren't included.
  - Because of these gaps, this is not a complete top five of U.S.-listed companies.
- **Data vintage:** I pulled USAspending after the cutoff, so records added after the cutoff may be included. LDA records were filtered by posting date.
- **Contract matching:** matching was by recipient name, not by unique entity ID (UEI), so a $0 could mean contracts are booked under other entity names.
- **Backlog:** I only did a keyword search, and none for CVS. I didn't check Stellantis entities other than FCA US.

**P09: Four-Regime Rotation research memo**

**Status:** The recommendation is resolved, with the caveats noted below. Growth was **falling** and inflation was **falling**, so the map prescribes **100% TLT**. This is the initial recommendation because no prior regime was supplied.
- **Decision time:** 2026-09-30 16:00 America/New_York (month-end convention)
- **Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z
- **Data retrieved:** 2026-10-16 at about 19:48 UTC. That's after the cutoff, but the snapshots are pinned to the 2026-09-30 vintage, so nothing published later was used.

**Conventions (set before computing and kept fixed)**
- **Growth series:** FRED/ALFRED `INDPRO`, US industrial production index, monthly, seasonally adjusted.
- **Inflation series:** FRED/ALFRED `CPIAUCSL`, CPI for all urban consumers, monthly, seasonally adjusted.
- **Vintage and lag rule:** use the ALFRED vintage dated 2026-09-30 and its latest observation, which is August 2026 for both series. Release lag is handled by using whatever was published in that vintage.
- **Transformation:** year-over-year % change of the latest month minus year-over-year % change three months earlier (Aug-26 vs May-26). Every value comes from the same vintage, and year-ago months are matched by date.
- **Sign rule:** a difference above 0 means rising and below 0 means falling. A difference of exactly 0.00 percentage points counts as falling and gets flagged; that didn't happen here.
- **Missing release:** use the latest earlier observation in that vintage. If the needed values can't be recovered, the regime stays unresolved. The vintage has a blank for October 2025 CPI, which is consistent with the 2025 government-shutdown gap, but no calculation needed that month.
- **Initialization:** with no prior state, the first month-end classification sets the allocation.

**Calculation (vintage 2026-09-30)**

| Series | Aug-26 | Aug-25 | YoY Aug-26 | May-26 | May-25 | YoY May-26 | Difference | Sign |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INDPRO | 103.0682 | 101.6247 | 1.42% | 102.6396 | 100.9655 | 1.66% | −0.24 pp | Falling |
| CPIAUCSL | 334.131 | 323.291 | 3.35% | 333.979 | 320.62 | 4.17% | −0.81 pp | Falling |

**P11, Google PageRank: status UNRESOLVED.** There's no top-10 recommendation and no S&P 500 scores.

- **Template:** P11
- **Common research cutoff:** 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z
- **Reference close:** 2026-10-15 16:00 America/New_York
- **Memo prepared:** 2026-10-16 about 19:50 UTC (retrieval time, not evidence time)

**Why it's unresolved (insufficient evidence, not verified absence)**
1. **Universe:** I didn't retrieve or freeze a dated S&P 500 constituent snapshot as of the cutoff. Without one, there's no node list.
2. **Cycle:** By the cutoff (mid-October 2026), the calendar Q3 2026 reporting season had only just started. Mostly large banks and a few early reporters had published. So Q3 is incomplete and can't be used, and the latest completed cycle would be calendar Q2 2026 (reports roughly mid-July to late August 2026, with off-calendar fiscal filers mapped to the quarter their period ends in). I didn't verify through primary sources that every constituent had reported for that cycle, so the cycle completion cutoff isn't established.
3. **Corpus:** The method needs about 500 earnings releases plus prepared remarks and company-published transcripts. That's thousands of primary documents, each needing a URL, a publication date, and mention extraction. I retrieved none of them. Many companies also don't publish their own transcripts, and that coverage gap wasn't measured either.
4. **Entity mapping and edges:** No mentions were extracted, so there's no edge list, no mention-to-ticker evidence, and no exclusion ledger.
5. **Computation:** PageRank wasn't run on any S&P 500 graph. Running it without the inputs above would mean making up scores.

The core reason is that this corpus can't be gathered and mapped auditably within a 600-second budget. A familiar-company list would not be a valid substitute.

**Claim-to-evidence ledger:** It's empty. No decision-driving facts were verified. The cycle-timing point in item 2 is based on the usual reporting calendar, and I haven't verified it from sources.

**Fixed conventions for any future run:**
- Damping is 0.85.
- Teleportation is uniform over all frozen nodes, and dangling-node mass is redistributed uniformly.
- Scores
Reviewed source excerpt · bytes 2200–3718
 sum to 1.
- Power iteration runs until the L1 change between rounds is at most 1e-10.
- Ranking is by raw score, highest first. Exact ties go to the ascending ticker, with each share class counted as its own ticker (for example, `GOOGL` and `GOOG` are separate nodes).
- Edges are unweighted and directed, with repeat mentions collapsed to one. Self-mentions, subsidiaries and ambiguous names are excluded, and analyst questions don't create edges.

**What I built (in the case folder `/workspace/grok-bot-native-P11-trial-1-20261016`, which I created new for this task):**
- `pagerank.py` implements the conventions above. It reads `nodes.txt` (one ticker per line) and `edges.csv` (source, destination pairs), so it's ready for a real corpus.
- `demo_nodes.txt`, `demo_edges.csv` and `demo_output_ILLUSTRATIVE.txt` are a five-node example with placeholder tickers AAA through EEE. It's **illustrative only and not an S&P 500 result.** It converged in 14 rounds with an L1 change of 8.08e-11, and the scores summed to 1. AAA and BBB tie at 0.4458, and CCC, DDD and EEE (the isolated node) are each at 0.0361.

**Conditional policy going forward:** The recommendation only becomes valid once three things are in place: a frozen universe, a cycle verified as complete, and an auditable edge list. With those, the top 10 by PageRank would each get 10%. After that, it would change only when a new earnings cycle is complete.

I didn't place any orders, change any accounts, deploy any schedules or start any monitoring.

Entry screen applied. Window: (2026-09-16T03:15:00Z, 2026-10-16T03:15:00Z], keyed to public disclosure availability (filing publication/receipt date), not trade date. Predeclared conventions (fixed for this trial; declared before any ranking or conclusion)

Consensus provider and vintage: timestamped point-in-time vendor consensus snapshots (e.g., a licensed estimates feed such as FactSet Estimates or Refinitiv I/B/E/S) with publication/availability timestamps at or before the decision time. Current consensus pages, analyst commentary, and narrative enthusiasm are not acceptable substitutes.
Forecast period: FY1 consensus EPS (current fiscal year) per company.
30-day endpoints: T1 = 2026-08-31 16:00 ET; T0 = 2026-09-30 16:00 ET.
Upward-revision strength: (C0 − C1) / |C1|, where C0 and C1 are FY1 consensus EPS at T0 and T1; exclude the company if C1 = 0 or either vintage is missing.
Upward-revision breadth: share of analysts covering the company at both endpoints whose individual FY1 EPS estimate increased over the window; require at least five covering analysts.
Normalization: cross-sectional z-scores of strength, breadth, and momentum across the qualifying universe, using only information available at T0.
Six-month momentum: total return from the 2026-03-31 close to the 2026-09-30 close on dividend- and split-adjusted prices.
Composite score: 0.40 × z(strength) + 0.30 × z(breadth) + 0.30 × z(momentum); rank descending.

Cycle: latest completed cycle = calendar Q2 2026 (period ended 2026-06-30). Justification: Q3 2026 reporting had only begun by the cutoff (see ledger). No incomplete Q3 subset was used.
Fiscal-calendar policy: for non-calendar fiscal reporters, use the earnings release for the fiscal quarter ending closest to 2026-06-30, published by the cutoff; such names are flagged, not silently dropped.
Sources: primary-source company earnings releases, prepared remarks, and company-published call transcripts only. Transcript Q&A counts only for the reporting company's own representatives; analyst questions never create edges. Unavailable primary transcripts are logged as corpus gaps.
Edges: A→B when reporting company A explicitly mentions company B, confidently mapped to an eligible universe ticker; one unweighted directed edge per ordered pair per cycle; self-mentions, generic words, subsidiaries (never converted to parents), and ambiguous entities excluded.
